| Chat tools with no guard policy: `conversation_search`, `recent_chats`, `todo_write`, `tool_search`, `question`, `file_read_range`, `browser_get_content`, `test_run`, `git_init`, `github_create_repo`, `browser_autofill_job_application`                 | Wire     | `ToolExecutionGuard::validate_tool_call` refuses a tool with no policy, so each failed on every call in the app. Each has a policy in `sys/security/tool_guard.rs`: the first six Low, `browser_get_content` Medium like `browser_get_dom_snapshot`, the last four behind approval. `test_run` splices its filter into a shell command, so the guard holds the filter to plain characters. `every_tool_the_chat_can_advertise_has_a_guard_policy` in `sys/commands/chat/tools.rs` enumerates the registry and the fallback list.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Chat tools whose policy refused their own schema: `ui_click`, `ui_type` and the four other `ui_` verbs (`app`), `api_call` (`query_params`, `auth`, `timeout_ms`), `db_query` (`connection_id`, which the registry requires), `physical_scrape` (`wait_ms`) | Wire     | The guard refuses a parameter its policy does not name, so a call that followed the schema failed. Each policy names what the schema declares; `ui_click` and `ui_type` lost the top-level `x`, `y` and `delay_ms` nothing reads. Chat `api_call` builds its request with `build_api_request` in `core/agi/api_tools_impl.rs`, the same as a goal run, so `query_params`, `auth`, `timeout_ms` and an object body are sent rather than dropped. `the_guard_accepts_every_advertised_tool_called_by_its_own_schema` builds a call from each advertised schema, required parameters only and then all of them, and asserts the guard accepts it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `format_file` chat tool                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Cut      | Advertised in the registry and the fallback list with no executor arm and no policy, so the model was told to call a tool that answered "Unknown tool". `file_write` formats what it writes. The `format_file` function in `sys/commands/code_search.rs` stays for that.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Quick Query model picker                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Wire     | It listed Managed Cloud auto models in every mode and wrote the choice to a store the send never read. It lists the shared model store's models admitted for the active conversation's execution boundary and selects through that store, as the main picker does.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Chat `db_query`, `db_execute`, `db_transaction_begin`, `db_transaction_commit`, `db_transaction_rollback`                                                                                                                                                   | Cut      | The chat executor ran the model's SQL on the app's own SQLite store with no account, execution-boundary or superseded filter, so a BYOK or Cloud chat could read Local-only chats and other accounts. Of the 40 readable tables, 37 hold one person's data (chats, memory, project instructions, goals, browser history, workflows, usage figures, MCP server environment) with no account or boundary column the model's SQL is held to; `agent_templates`, `automation_benchmarks` and `process_benchmarks` are the only ones safe on every boundary and no chat needs them. `db_execute` also read `query` where the schema says `sql`, and `db_transaction_begin` opened a transaction on the one shared connection. `core/llm/tool_executor/db_tools.rs` is deleted; `NOT_OFFERED_IN_CHAT` in `sys/commands/chat/tools.rs` keeps the five out of the chat schema, `tool_search` and the executor, which answers a call by name with a refusal. The registry entries and guard policies stay for goal runs, where `core/agi/executors/database_executor.rs` reaches a database the person connected through `connection_id`. Past conversations stay reachable through `conversation_search` and `recent_chats`. |
| Chat `llm_reason`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Cut      | It sent the prompt to a fixed provider's fast model with no trust mode, which the router treats as Local-only, read `model`, `depth` and `max_tokens` the guard refuses, and ignored the declared `context`. A chat model reasons in its own reply. The registry entry stays for the goal planner (`core/agi/planner.rs`, `core/agi/executors/llm_executor.rs`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Tool arguments seen by the hook gate                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Wire     | `execute_tool_call` handed the PreToolUse hook the raw arguments and promoted aliases afterwards, so `{"cmd": …}` passed a hook written against `command`. `normalized_arguments` runs once, before the gate; the hook, the guard, folder consent and the tool read the same map, including the `file_list` default path.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Local stand-ins for `mcp__filesystem__read_text_file` and `list_allowed_directories`                                                                                                                                                                        | Wire     | Both returned before the capability toggle, the guard and the connector permission. They now sit after all three.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `physical_scrape`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Wire     | It fetched a model-chosen URL with no prompt, so a file read followed by a scrape could carry the file out in the query string. It asks first, like every other tool that sends a request where the model points (`api_call`, `api_download`, `api_upload`, `browser_navigate`, `git_clone`, `video_generate`). `selector` and `wait_ms` are gone from the guard and the schema: nothing applied either.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Safe and Plan mode tool list                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Wire     | The hand list in `sys/commands/tool_confirmation.rs` is replaced by `UNATTENDED_READ_ONLY_TOOLS`, six chat-only reading and planning tools, and two named research tools, `search_web` and `image_analyze`, which a person is there to watch and which keep their own guard policy and capability toggle. Gained: `file_read_binary`, `git_diff`, `git_log`, `memory_recall` and the rest of the unattended list, `file_read_range`, `conversation_search`, `recent_chats`, `tool_search`, `todo_write`, `question`. Lost: `db_query`. `skill` stays out. One test holds the list to exactly those three parts.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| Undeclared tool parameters                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Cut      | Guard policies no longer allow what no schema declares: `browser_click` `x`, `y`; `db_query` `params`; `git_clone` `branch`; `video_generate` `duration_secs`, `duration`, `input_image_url`; `schedule_recurring_task` `action_type`, `action_data`; `cancel_scheduled_task` `job_id`; `edit_exact_replace` `session_id`; `document_read`, `document_search` `path`; `productivity_create_task` `task`. The chat executor stopped reading `session_id` (six file tools), `job_id`, `action_type`, `action_data`, `input_image_url` and the raw `task` object. `productivity_create_task` now declares the six fields its executor reads. `every_parameter_a_policy_allows_is_one_the_tool_declares` holds the reverse direction.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `search_chat_history`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Wire     | `user_id` and `app_mode` were optional; left out, the search read every account in both modes. Both are required and an unknown mode is refused.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Agent task steps and the tool guard                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Wire     | `core/agent/executor.rs` ran a step past the hook and its own validators only. After the hook, a step now answers to the tool guard under the chat tool that covers it: `terminal_execute`, `file_read`, `file_write`, `browser_navigate`, `ui_screenshot`. Its own command and URL-scheme checks are gone as duplicates; its file checks stay, because they refuse `~/.ssh`, `~/.config` and system folders the guard allows. Waiting for an element and searching the screen have no guard rule: they take no path, URL or command. Each step gets a guard of its own, so the per-minute limits tuned for chat do not apply, and no approval is added: every step meets the unattended tool rule in `core/agent/autonomous.rs` before it is dispatched. That loop also holds a file step to the user's granted folders, so the step guard applies every path rule except that one. A step that opens a loopback or private address is now refused.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Opening a URL from an agent step                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Wire     | On Windows `core/agent/executor.rs` ran `cmd /C start "" <url>`, and `cmd` splits on `&`, so a query string could start a second program. Every platform now goes through `open::that_detached` (open 5.4.4), which hands the URL to the launcher as one argument or one environment value.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Standing approval for tools that send data where the model points                                                                                                                                                                                           | Cut      | `api_call`, `api_upload` and `physical_scrape` joined `NEVER_REMEMBERABLE`: an always-allow, a session approval or auto-approve no longer answers for them, and an unattended run cannot use them. `api_download`, `browser_navigate` and `git_clone` stay rememberable: tests in `core/agi/executor.rs` and `core/agent/autonomous.rs` rely on a stored allow for the first two, and navigation happens in a browser the person watches.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Folders granted for reading                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Wire     | A read-only folder consent never reached the tool guard, so `file_read` on a just-approved folder outside the home folder failed. The guard holds a read set (every session folder) beside the modify set; read tools check the read set, tools that change files and the filesystem MCP server the modify set only.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Order of the chat tool gates                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Wire     | Folder consent ran before the capability toggle, the guard and the Safe/Plan gate, so a call that would be refused still asked for a folder and could leave a remembered grant. The order is now mode gate, capability, guard pass one (every rule except granted folders), folder consent, guard pass two (granted folders, no second rate-limit charge), approval.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Capability toggles                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Wire     | `sys/commands/capabilities.rs` mapped 43 tools. `tool_switch` now places every registry tool under a capability or names it always-on with the reason, and a test fails when one is neither. File operations now covers `file_read_range`, the edit and search tools, undo, checkpoints and `mcp__filesystem__*`; Terminal covers `test_run`; Web search covers `search_web` and `physical_scrape`; Git covers every `git_*`, `worktree_*` and `github_create_repo`. Image, video and background-agent tools are always-on because their historic toggles defaulted to off and no screen sets them. No Settings screen writes these toggles today (`setFeature` has no caller outside tests).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Capability switches outside chat, and in what the model is offered                                                                                                                                                                                          | Wire     | Only the chat executor read the switches, and a tool whose capability was off was still offered to the model. `CapabilityState::tool_refusal` is the one question; it is asked when the chat tool list is built (`sys/commands/chat/tool_config.rs`, including deferred MCP tools and the provider's `web_search`), by `tool_search`, by the unattended approval gate that every agent task step, background agent and unattended goal run passes (`unattended_tool_approval`), and before the approval prompt and the cache on an attended goal run. A capability that is off outranks a stored always-allow. Shell commands and scripts a person schedules themselves do not pass these gates.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Capability switches in computer use, the action router and the embedded MCP server                                                                                                                                                                          | Wire     | With Computer control off, `computer_use_execute_opa_task` refuses to start and a running session ends at its next observation or action. The action router asks the same question for each routed call. The embedded MCP server leaves a tool whose capability is off out of the tool list it returns and refuses it on call: `agi_bash` under Terminal, `agi_research` under Web search. The goal planner is told which tools are off by the goal run that starts it (`AGICore::withheld_tools`, `WithheldTools` in `core/agi/planner.rs`): it offers none of them to the planning model, asks for one more plan when a step names one, and then fails with the capability's label before any step runs.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `core/llm/function_executor.rs` and `ToolExecutor::get_tool_definitions`                                                                                                                                                                                    | Cut      | Nothing constructed `FunctionExecutor` or referred to it outside its own file, and it was the only caller of `get_tool_definitions`, a tool list built without the capability switches.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Agent commands judged as argument vectors                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Wire     | A step spawns the program with its arguments and no shell, but was judged as shell text, so `git commit -m "a; b"` was refused. `ToolExecutionGuard::validate_argv` applies the blocklists to the vector and the full shell rule only when the vector names a shell.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `mcp__filesystem__read_text_file` stand-in                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Wire     | It read a file of any size and cut PDF text at a byte index, which panics inside a multi-byte character. It refuses a file over the read limit before reading and cuts text on a character boundary, as `file_read` does.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Schedule a task stores (`core/scheduler/schedule.rs`)                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Wire     | The task form sent `{ type, runAt, interval }` and `scheduler_add_job` read only `cronExpression`: "Run once" was stored as daily 09:00 UTC for ever, an edit from Daily to Weekly saved nothing and still said "Task updated", and the form's own five-field cron example was refused. `TaskSchedule` mirrors the form field for field (once with `runAt`; hourly, daily, weekly, monthly with `timeOfDay`, `weekday`, `dayOfMonth`; custom cron; `timezone`), refuses what it cannot store with the reason, and is the only thing that turns a schedule into a next run. `cron_schedule` there takes five fields or six. A job stored before has no `plan` and runs on its UTC cron as before, shown as that.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| Time zone of a scheduled task                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Wire     | Every schedule was read in UTC. The form sends the computer's IANA zone, the next run is walked on that zone's wall clock (a time a clock change skips runs an hour later, a repeated hour runs once), and the form and list state the zone. A task made in chat carries no zone and follows the computer's.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| One-time scheduled task                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Wire     | A task set to run once runs once and becomes Completed, shown as Finished with its switch off; a new time from the form puts it back on. One that could not start (Cloud task in Local mode, or due more than 7 days ago) is paused with the reason, and turning it on runs it at the next poll.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Description and model on a scheduled task                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Wire/Cut | Description is sent and stored on create and edit. The model picker is cut: a scheduled run is a goal run on the router's default and nothing read `modelId`. `status` is no longer editable through `scheduler_update_job`; the toggle is the way. Unknown edit fields are refused, not dropped.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `schedule_reminder` and `schedule_recurring_task` chat tools                                                                                                                                                                                                | Wire     | Both built five-field strings for a parser that needs six fields, with weekdays one off and local times read as UTC, so every call failed. They store the same `TaskSchedule` as the form and announce the job to open windows. An interval is accepted only when a clock keeps it exactly (5 to 30 minutes, 1 to 12 hours that divide the day, daily, weekly); "every 45 minutes" is refused instead of bunching at the hour. "every monday 9am" parses without "at". A task runs at most every 5 minutes.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Task stopped after repeated failures                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Wire     | The third failure in a row set Failed silently, the list said "Paused" and the toggle was refused. It now sends a notification with the last error that opens the task, the list and card say Stopped with the reason, and "Turn back on" (the toggle command) restarts the count. A Cloud task due while the app is in Local mode, or signed out, is recorded as skipped with that reason, moves to its next run and is not a failure; the first skip notifies, later ones do not. Scheduler refusals reach the toast in the scheduler's own words.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Scheduled shell command, script, webhook and workflow jobs                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Cut      | Only `scheduler_add_job` called by hand could create them; the task form creates AI tasks and chat creates reminders and AI tasks. Shell jobs were validated as `code_execute` and could not run; script jobs ran `sh -c` behind a first-word allowlist with nobody to approve. The command takes `name`, `prompt`, `schedule`, `description` only; the kinds stay in the enum so a stored row loads and can be deleted, and a run of one fails with that explanation. `validate_shell_command`, the allowlist and the shell runner are deleted. `src/features/reminders` (four files, on the reachability guard's unreachable list, the only caller of the old shape) is deleted with the store actions only it used (`addJob`, `removeJob`, `pauseJob`, `resumeJob`, and the unused `toggleJob`, `runJobNow`, `updateJobOnBackend` and job selectors) and the two commands that leaves without a caller, `scheduler_pause_job` and `scheduler_resume_job`; the toggle is the one way to pause and resume. `src/__tests__/scheduler.test.ts`, which called a mocked `invoke` and asserted on the same mock, is deleted.                                                                                             |
| Relative paths at the tool guard                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Wire     | The guard resolved `src/a.ts` against the process directory while the tool opened it under the project. The chat executor makes every path argument the guard judges absolute once (`ToolExecutionGuard::path_arguments`, plus `multi_edit` and checkpoint paths, `~/` to home), and the guard refuses a relative path. Goal runs resolve the same way against the open project before the approval, the cache, the hook and the guard (`ToolExecutionGuard::resolve_path_arguments`, one function for chat and goal runs); agent file steps have `core::agent::executor::step_in_project` for the agent loop to call before its granted-folder check. With no project open a relative path is refused with "Give the full path". A connector tool's value under a path-like name is judged as a path only when it is rooted; otherwise only `..` is refused.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Argument-vector rule for agent commands (`validate_argv`)                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Wire     | `python3 -I -c`, `find -exec` and `osascript -e` passed and `mv /a /b` was refused. Code on the command line (python `-c`, node `-e`, perl, ruby, lua, php, osascript, awk `system(`, git `-c` and helper overrides) is refused outright, since the shell rule passes those forms; a shell or a command runner anywhere (xargs, env, busybox, nohup, sudo, timeout and others, find `-exec`) is judged as shell text; file commands are judged by where their rooted paths point.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Agent `ReadFile` step                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Wire     | It returned "Read N bytes from path" and dropped the content. It returns the content, cut at the chat read limit on a character boundary, and refuses a file over the chat size cap.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `method` on `api_call`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Wire     | The schema gave GET as the default and the builder required it. A missing or null method is GET, the parameter is no longer required, and a non-string method is refused.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Plan with no model (`AGIPlanner::generate_basic_plan`)                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Cut      | With no model able to plan, the planner built steps from words in the prompt: a screenshot for any prompt whose tools matched "ui", "click" or "automate" as substrings ("build", "guide"), which a scheduled run then reported as finished without errors. The fallback is deleted; `invoke_plan_candidate` returns the reason (no model in the boundary, the model's error, or the timeout) and the run fails with it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Stopping a scheduled run                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Wire     | A run could not be stopped, and deleting or pausing its task left the agent working and later notifying for a task that was gone. Each run has a stop request (`RunStop`); `scheduler_stop_run` asks, the agent wait cancels the agent, and the run is recorded as stopped: not a run, not a failure, no notice. `scheduler_remove_job`, the chat `cancel_scheduled_task` tool and pausing through the toggle all stop it, and a run whose task was deleted records and announces nothing. `scheduler_running_jobs` and the `scheduler:run_started` event show Running with a Stop on the task row and card.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| Scheduled runs in Monitor                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Cut      | A scheduled run's goal events listed it in Monitor, whose Cancel asks an engine that never held it and whose restart check marked the live run interrupted. Goals with the scheduler's `scheduled_` prefix are kept out of `agentTaskStore`; the Scheduled list owns the run.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Run history (`scheduler_get_history`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Wire     | Runs were recorded and nothing read them. A task row in Scheduled opens to its last result and its last five runs with each failure or skip reason, and a notification that routes to a task opens that row. The row also has run-now and edit, which only the Tasks panel card had.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Run now on a one-time task                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Wire     | A manual run before the task's time marked it Completed, so it never ran at its time. It stays Active until a run at or after its time.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Late scheduled runs                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Wire     | A run started late, after the app was closed or the computer slept, said so only in the success notice. The job records when it had been due (`last_run_was_due`); the notice, for a success or a failure, and the task row state it. The quit prompt said an interrupted run "runs again at its next time"; it starts again when the app reopens, within 7 days, and says so.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| "Daily news summary" example                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Cut      | A scheduled prompt with no tool keyword plans one `llm_reason` step, and `search_web` needs an approval nobody is there to give, so the example could only invent news. Replaced with "Daily planning questions", which the run can do.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Delete on the scheduled task card                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Wire     | It deleted from `onClick`. It asks through `useConfirmAction`, naming what stops and what is removed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |

## 2026-10-11: Electron desktop inventory

Checked by reading the code and by unit tests; nothing here was exercised in a running shell.

| Capability                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Decision            | Evidence and retained path                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Approved folders, allowed programs and standing permissions in Settings                                                                                                                                        | Wire                | `LocalAccessSection` was mounted only for the Tauri local-mode host, so the Electron shell could not show or remove an approved folder or edit the allow-list its own native dialogs point to. `CapabilitiesSection` now mounts it for any desktop host, and it lists every standing permission with a Withdraw action over `permission_list` and `permission_revoke`. The local-models panel inside it still renders only for a local-mode host and no longer calls the runtime elsewhere.                                                                                                                                                                         |
| Voice shortcut while Quick Ask is up or the main window is hidden                                                                                                                                              | Wire                | The Quick Ask panel has no preload, so the press reached no listener. The press now always lands in the main window, raised if needed; a closed window is reopened with a notice to press again. Attaching the preload to the panel was rejected: it would hand the panel the whole runtime bridge, and a native dialog raised over a panel that hides on blur cannot be answered.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Agent question with only a phone connected                                                                                                                                                                     | Wire                | The phone protocol has no answer action, so a connected phone no longer counts as able to answer (`main.ts`, `answersUserInput`). With no window that can show the question, the CLI is told there is no answer rather than left waiting.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Folder dropped on the window                                                                                                                                                                                   | Wire                | `workspaceDrop.ts` reports each grant and `main.ts` broadcasts `workspace-changed`; Settings, the attach dialog, the command dialog and the Code folder list reread on it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Pairing-bridge start failure                                                                                                                                                                                   | Wire                | `BrowserPairingState.unavailableReason` carries why the bridge is not listening and the Capabilities panel prints it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| Coding-session capability flags                                                                                                                                                                                | Wire                | `readDeveloperRuntimeStatus` reported every feature until a CLI had answered. It now reports none until `initialize` has been answered, then derives each from the handshake through one typed table (`CAPABILITY_FROM_HANDSHAKE`), so a capability cannot be added without saying what it rests on.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| Session held by another writer                                                                                                                                                                                 | Wire                | The CLI's writer-conflict refusal (`-32011`) surfaces as `thread-held`; the panel names the holder and offers `developer_thread_takeover` behind a confirmation that says the other program loses the session. Gated on the CLI's `writerLease`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Local session archive                                                                                                                                                                                          | Wire                | `developer_thread_archive` reaches `thread/archive`. The app-server has no unarchive method, so the confirmation and the notice afterwards name `agi session unarchive <id>`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Commit from the local review pane                                                                                                                                                                              | Cut (not built)     | Neither the app-server protocol nor the host git path has a commit method, and `git commit` runs repository hooks. The pane now says commits happen in the terminal. Recommendation: add `checkpoint/commit` to the app-server first, under the same refusal of code-running git config, then wire it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Stripe checkout, portal and top-up; connector sign-in for providers the window does not host                                                                                                                   | Wire                | The shell opens these in the browser on purpose (`continueInBrowser`), shows a "Continue in your browser" notice, and rereads the plan or the connection each time the window regains focus. The navigation allow-list is unchanged; the hosts that stay in the window are named once in the contract (`HOST_IN_WINDOW_SIGN_IN_HOSTS`) and a test holds the shell's policy to it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Downloads from this site's own API routes; printing 2FA backup codes                                                                                                                                           | Wire                | A same-origin `/api/` link or skill file is saved from the window, where the session is, instead of being sent to a browser that has none. Backup codes print from a hidden frame; the shell refuses every new window.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Camera in voice mode, the browser folder picker, web push                                                                                                                                                      | Hide in the shell   | The shell carries no camera entitlement or usage string and grants the microphone only, refuses the browser file-system permission, and exposes the Push API with no push service behind it. Each control is withheld there: the camera toggle and the folder picker are not rendered (approved folders and the local-folder attach replace the picker), and the notifications row says the desktop app notifies through macOS. `safeClipboard.readText` has no caller, so there was no control to hide.                                                                                                                                                            |
| Network for a local command                                                                                                                                                                                    | Wire                | The sandbox's allow branch was unreachable. The native approval now carries an "Allow network access for this command" box, unticked by default and good for that one run; a command that is not prompted keeps the network blocked.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `developer_session_resume`, `workspace_snapshot`, `window_open` and `browser_pairing_uninstall_host` dispatcher cases; `planWindowOpen`; on-device inference in the Electron tree; the page-side Bypass dialog | Cut                 | No caller. `computer_take_over` stays, because the page's computer use controls take over through it; the native menu, tray and shortcut call the service directly. The dispatcher still refuses every local-inference command by name. The native Bypass confirmation in main is the one that remains, because a page cannot answer it; declining it puts the picker back and returns the message to the composer. The pairing QR carries the pairing secret, so only the shell's own pairing window draws it; the page is told the short code, and the web panel's QR drawing does not render in this shell.                                                      |
| `file_stat` and `file_create_directory`                                                                                                                                                                        | Cut                 | No caller. Their dispatcher cases and capability entries are gone, and `file_stat` has left `FILESYSTEM_COMMANDS`; `file_create_directory` is still listed there, and the dispatcher test names it among the listed commands this shell does not answer. `file_glob` and `file_grep` stay, because the device steps `device_find_files` and `device_search_text` and the attach-from-folder search call them.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Non-macOS branches (bubblewrap, `taskkill`, the Windows environment allow-list)                                                                                                                                | Leave               | `electron-builder.yml` targets macOS only and nothing in this repository configures a Windows or Linux build of this shell. They are left in place and are not evidence of support.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| App-server `commands/list` and `commands/run`                                                                                                                                                                  | Recorded, not built | Recommendation: wire next. Slash commands in the local composer are the largest gap between this pane and the terminal.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| App-server `hooks/list` and `context/instructions`; installing, updating or removing skills and plugins                                                                                                        | Recorded, not built | Skills and plugins are listed, switched on or off and consented to from the Skills and plugins popover in the session header (`LocalExtensionsControl.tsx`), through `developer_skills_list`, `developer_skill_set_enabled`, `developer_skill_consent`, `developer_plugins_list` and `developer_plugin_set_enabled`. Recommendation: add hooks and the folder's instructions to that list read-only, since hooks run programs the user should be able to see.                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| App-server `mcp/login`, and `mcp/list` as a server list                                                                                                                                                        | Recorded, not built | Recommendation: leave unwired. This shell reports `localMcp: false` under D-2026-09-15-04; a list of servers or a sign-in to one reverses that decision and belongs with it. `mcp/list` is read only to find project servers that wait for approval (next row).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Approving a project MCP server from the app (`mcp/approval/read`, `mcp/approval/grant`)                                                                                                                        | Wire                | On a CLI that advertises `mcpApproval`, the session list names each waiting server per folder and the Code rail shows one line and a Review action. `developer_mcp_approval_review` reads the launch in main and shows its parts, then the CLI's question, in a native dialog that defaults to Cancel; a yes returns the fingerprint of the launch shown, and one refused as stale (`-32012`) is asked afresh from the launch the refusal carries.
