The Tauri app passes `None`, which restarts as before; its lane decides whether to supply a check.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| What an approval records                                             | Wire                                                 | The hash that was compared is the hash recorded (`record_fingerprint`), never one taken again while recording. The terminal review hashes before it asks, looks again after a yes, and asks again about the launch as it is now when it changed, up to three times. The launch in parts also carries `oauthScope`, `oauthClientId` and `oauthRedirectUri`, which the hash already pinned for a remote server, and `oauthClientSecretSet`, which says a client secret is configured and pinned without ever carrying its value.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| What an approval shows and what it hashes                            | Wire                                                 | Arguments and URLs were shown and hashed whole, so `postgresql://u:pw@h/db` and `--token abc` were on screen and in the hash. Each value now has a shown form and a pinned form. A value an option or a variable names (`--api-key x`, `--api-key=x`, `NAME=x`) is read by that name through `agiworkforce_sandbox_policy::shown_value` and `pinned_value`, the same classes the environment uses: a credential by name is neither shown nor pinned, a name that may be one is not shown and is pinned by a digest of its value, an endpoint is shown and pinned as where it points, and anything else whole. Any value that reads as a URL is reduced to where it points wherever it stands. `-p` straight before or attached to a password is hidden for `sshpass`, `redis-cli` and the MySQL clients only. The reduced forms are what `McpApprovalLaunch.args`, `url` and the sign-in URLs carry. An OAuth client secret is no longer hashed, only whether one is set. The value after an option whose name reads as a credential is not shown and is pinned by a digest, since `--auth none` and `--key ed25519` are switches. `McpApprovalLaunch.askedAtEveryStart` says a launch has something that cannot be checked, from the same entries that keep its approval from outliving the run, so a client need not read that from wording a file name could imitate. A launch with no credential in it hashes byte for byte as before; one that had a credential in an argument, a URL or an endpoint variable asks once more.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| What the consent says about the environment and what the server gets | Wire                                                 | The engine dropped `NODE_OPTIONS`, the `LD_` and `DYLD_` loader variables, `PYTHONPATH`, `RUBYLIB`, `PERL5LIB` and every proxy variable an entry set, while the consent showed and pinned them. The filter is now one exported function, `agiworkforce_mcp::manifest_var_is_passed`, which the spawn and the consent both call. The loader variables stay withheld: each loads code from a place the command line does not name, which no pinned file covers. The list also holds `BASH_ENV`, `ENV`, `NODE_PATH`, `RUBYOPT`, `PERL5OPT`, `PYTHONSTARTUP`, the Java option variables and `DOTNET_STARTUP_HOOKS`, which were passed with their value pinned and the file that value names not. A command line that assigns one of them all the same, through `env`, a shell string or any other wrapper, is marked as a launch that cannot be checked and is asked at every start. The consent lists them as "set in the config but not passed to the server" (`McpApprovalEnv.notPassed`) and pins nothing of them. A proxy an entry sets is passed, shown as where it points and pinned: the parent's proxy variables are not inherited, so the entry is the only way a server behind a proxy reaches the network. The Tauri app uses the same engine and now passes a config-set proxy too.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Reading the text files a project supplies                            | Wire                                                 | `settings/read`, which a read-only connection may call, read `.agiworkforce/instructions.md` with `read_to_string`, following a committed link to any file and never ending on `/dev/zero`; the same read fed the model a project's memory, rules and skills. `project_text::read_within(root, path)` reads such a file through the sandbox-policy crate's `read_file_within`, the one reader for these files: only where it lands, once every link is followed, on a regular file inside the project, opened there without following a link and without blocking, and up to 1 MiB. A file that is absent is nothing to report; any other refusal is logged once for that file. A link that stays inside the project is followed, since `CLAUDE.md` linked to `AGENTS.md` is common. It is used for the project instruction file in `surfaces.rs`, the project and local memory files and the project rules folder in `memory.rs`, a project's skill files in `skills.rs`, and the `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and `.agiworkforce/instructions.md` that `compaction.rs` puts in the system prompt, each confined to the folder it was found in. Left as they were: the user's own files in the home and config folders, plugin skills (already confined by `plugin_path_stays_within_root`), and the JSON manifests in `features/hooks` and `features/plugins`, which are parsed and never forwarded. An `@` mention and the repository index judged a credential file by the name they were given, so `notes.txt` linked to `.env` was inlined or indexed; both now ask `path_security::reads_a_credential_file`, which judges the name and where a read of it lands, and the index puts that ahead of every other class so that naming the file does not bring it in.         |
| Consent to the skills a project supplies                             | Wire, moved                                          | The consent was a `.consent` file inside the project's skills folder that named one path, so a repository could ship its own for a predictable checkout path and nothing of the skills was pinned; skills of the project's own plugins loaded with no consent at all. It is now kept in the user's config folder (`project-skills-consent.json`), by resolved project path, as a digest of every skill the project supplies, its skills folder and its plugins' skills alike: each file's place, content and package content. A skill added, removed or edited asks again, on a terminal through the same prompt and over the app-server through the same `skills/consent` call. `skills/list` returns that digest as `projectSkillsDigest`, and a grant sends it back as `digest`: the digest recorded is the one that was shown and compared, and a grant whose digest is no longer the current one records nothing and is refused with the stale code the MCP approval uses (-32012), carrying the list as it is now. A record inside the project is no longer read. The Tauri app keeps the same idea in its settings store.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Transcript when the context stops matching                           | Keep the transcript; sidecar and set-aside store cut | `ManagedSession::reconcile_transcript` and the save path dropped the stored transcript whenever its fingerprint no longer matched the context, which lost the only copy of the history to any edit the transcript was not told of. Now there are three cases. A context cut shorter than an entry was cleared or rewound, and what the entry held is dropped, as the user asked. A conversation replaced by another on purpose calls `ManagedSession::conversation_replaced` and starts with no transcript; the one such path is `sessions::import_hosted_session` (a hosted conversation written over a stored one); `conversations::restore_into_session`, the other, had only test callers and is cut with its tests, since a legacy conversation is loaded into a new session. Resume, `/load` and the Local-to-cloud continuation adopt the stored session together with its messages, so they replace nothing under a transcript. Anything else, a context as long as the entry that reads differently, keeps every message of the transcript and goes on showing it; only that entry's fingerprint is brought up to date, and a warning naming the session is logged once. It is not frozen instead: a transcript that stopped taking later summaries would lose every turn summarized afterwards. The `.conflict-transcript-<hash>` sidecar and the `set_aside` store that followed it are cut: nothing could show them safely, so nothing read them. Transcript messages are validated as conversation messages are. The legacy branch that matched any conversation when an entry had no fingerprint is removed, since no build wrote such an entry. A pruning pass that adds or drops messages is recorded as a compaction instead of leaving stale fingerprints. |

## 2026-10-11: Tauri public HTTP tools

Retained Tauri code only. Applies to `api_call`, `api_upload`, `api_download`, the page scrape and the renderer's public API request, which share `ApiClient::public()` in `sys/api/client.rs`.

| Capability                                      | Decision | Evidence and retained path                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| ----------------------------------------------- | -------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Pinned transport for every public request       | Wire     | `api_call` judged the host with one lookup and then sent on a client that resolved again. All five paths now send through `PublicHttpClient::without_redirects`: each hop is resolved once, judged, and dialed at the judged address. System and environment proxies are not used on this client, so a network that requires a proxy will not reach these tools.                                   |
| Redirects                                       | Wire     | Followed by the client itself, at most 10 hops, each judged and pinned again. A hop to another origin keeps only Accept, Accept-Language, Content-Language, Content-Type and User-Agent, so no credential header crosses, whatever its name. A 307 or 308 that would replay a request body to another origin is not followed; the redirect is returned to the caller.                              |
| Automatic retry                                 | Cut      | POST, PUT, PATCH, DELETE and OPTIONS are sent once. GET and HEAD are retried on a connect error or a 502, 503 or 504, three attempts in all. `download_file` no longer retries on any other failure.                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| Body limits                                     | Wire     | Request body 10 MB, response body read 10 MB with `truncated` on the result, file upload and download 100 MB. A larger download is refused and nothing is saved. The scrape gained a 30 s timeout.                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Query strings in logs                           | Cut      | The public path logs origin and path only.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| Chat `api_upload` and `api_download` parameters | Wire     | Chat ignored the declared `auth`, `field_name` and `fields` that goal runs honoured. Both now read them through `build_api_upload` and `build_api_download` in `core/agi/api_tools_impl.rs`, with the same limits and cross-origin rule.                                                                                                                                                           |
| Scrape `selector`                               | Cut      | The scrape read a selector and reported it as requested without applying it. The read and the message are removed with the parameter.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Upload source path                              | Wire     | Chat checked the upload path and then reopened the raw spelling, so a relative path was read against the process directory and a link swapped in after the check was followed. The path is now resolved and checked once, and the client opens that path once without following a link (`open_file_without_following` in `sys/utils.rs`) and uploads from the handle. Goal runs use the same read. |
| Caller-supplied request headers                 | Cut      | Host, Content-Length, Transfer-Encoding, Connection, Upgrade, TE, Trailer, Keep-Alive, Expect and every Proxy- header are refused by name, as a plain header or as the API key header. The request is not sent.                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Refused address ranges                          | Wire     | The one judge in `sys/security/egress_policy.rs` now also refuses 198.18.0.0/15, 192.0.0.0/24, fec0::/10, 100::/64 and 2001:db8::/32, and refuses 6to4 (2002::/16) and Teredo (2001::/32) whole, since no public service is reached only through either. The web policy list should gain the same ranges.                                                                                          |
| Download URL in goal-run progress and logs      | Cut      | Origin and path only, through the same `loggable_url` the client uses. Transport errors no longer carry the request URL.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |

## 2026-10-11: protected paths, patches and the sandbox

CLI and the shared sandbox-policy crate. Applies to the approval gate for file edits, `apply_patch`, and the Seatbelt and bubblewrap profiles the CLI and the retained Tauri code build.

| Capability                                                        | Decision | Evidence and retained path                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------- | -------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| One parser for a patch's targets                                  | Wire     | `apply_patch::patch_targets` asks git which files a patch changes (`git apply --numstat -z`, and again with `-R` for where a rename or copy comes from). The listing and the apply are the same command (`git_apply`): in the folder the patch applies in, with the repository named outright so git never looks for one: `GIT_DIR` and `GIT_WORK_TREE` are that folder when it holds `.git`, and `GIT_DIR` is the null device when it does not, which git reads as no repository. Git therefore cannot read a name through where the folder sits in a repository above it, guess a strip level from that, or skip a file and exit 0. A ceiling list (`GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES`) did this before and split at a `:` in the folder's name; checked with git 2.54 on a folder with a colon, a linked worktree's `.git` file, an empty `.git` folder and a nested folder. No `-p` is passed, because `-p1` refuses `--- x.rs`. The apply prints what it changed (`--apply --numstat`) and is an error unless that is the list that was checked. The approval gate, the patch tool's permission and freshness checks and the containment check all read the one list. A patch git cannot parse, or that names no file, is refused in every mode, Bypass included.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Reading patch targets off header lines                            | Cut      | Three copies (`tools/mod.rs`, `file_ops/mod.rs`, `apply_patch.rs`) stripped `a/` and `b/` as text. Git accepts C-quoted names, so `+++ "b/.husk\171/pre-commit"` matched nothing and wrote `.husky/pre-commit`. The content-hash permission key for a patch with no readable target went with them: such a patch is now refused.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| A bare absolute patch header                                      | Keep     | `--- /opt/x` is not an absolute path to git: it strips the leading slash as the `-p1` prefix and writes `opt/x` below the folder. It is judged as that relative path. `a//opt/x`, which git does read as absolute, is refused.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| One resolver for where a write lands                              | Wire     | `agiworkforce_sandbox_policy::landing_path` follows every link on a path, one whose target does not exist yet included, at the end of the path or in the middle, bounded at 40 hops. The protected-path gate, the Seatbelt and bubblewrap rules, the credential read denials, the CLI's workspace and instruction-folder checks and the patch containment check use it. A loop is an error: the gate treats it as protected and the path checks refuse it. The gate resolves the path as it was written, so `lnk/../config` with `lnk -> .git/info` is judged as `.git/config`, where the kernel writes it; the spelling with the dots taken out as text is a second candidate that can only add protection. The other places that normalise a path before a decision already resolved first or refuse `..` (`path_security::lexically_normalized` runs on the resolved path, `host_path` follows links component by component, `sandbox.rs` and `merge_conflicts::validate_conflict_path` refuse the component, `apply_patch::refuse_escaping_name` counts depth and then resolves).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `path_security::resolve_for_denylist`, `protected_paths::resolve` | Cut      | Two private resolvers. The first followed a dangling link only at the last component; the second not at all, so `notes.txt -> .git/hooks/pre-commit` read as an ordinary file.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| File tool writes through a link                                   | Cut      | `write_file`, `edit_file` and `multiedit` open the last component with `O_NOFOLLOW` on Unix, so a link put in place after the path was checked is refused. A dangling link is validated as the file it points at, so writing through one still works. Windows has no such flag and is unchanged. That guarded the last component only: with a prompt for `d/x` open, a sandboxed command still running could make `d` a link to `.git/hooks`. Where a write lands is now taken when the path is checked and compared again just before the write, and once the file is open, before it is emptied, the open file has to be the one at that place with no link on the way (`landing_when_checked`, `moved_since_checked`, `opened_where_approved`); `apply_patch` makes the comparison for each file before git is started. Left: on Windows only the comparison is made, and a swap inside the comparison and the open creates an empty file in the wrong place, which is then refused and not written.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| Hook managers' files in the sandbox                               | Wire     | Decided in review: `.husky`, `.githooks`, the lefthook files and `.pre-commit-config.yaml` are denied to sandboxed commands like `.git`. A hook manager's install cannot finish under the sandbox anyway, since it writes git's metadata; it is rerun with an approved escalation. The list is now each manager's documented names, read on 2026-10-11, 42 in all: lefthook's main file as YAML, TOML, JSON and JSONC under its plain name, with a leading dot and inside `.config`, and the `lefthook-local` file of each kind, which lefthook merges over the main one (before, `lefthook-local.yml` was written unasked in a mode that accepts edits and ran at the next commit); husky's version 4 files (`.huskyrc` and its `.json`, `.yaml`, `.yml`, `.js` forms, `husky.config.js`); overcommit's `.overcommit.yml`, `.local-overcommit.yml` and `.git-hooks`. Pre-commit documents one file. Left out: `package.json`, and simple-git-hooks' own files, which change nothing until its installer runs again and writes git's metadata. `.config/lefthook-local.*` is listed by the rule for the main file; lefthook's documentation does not say it looks there.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Protected entries at any depth, in any case                       | Wire     | Both Seatbelt profiles rule the entries other than `.git` by regex below the workspace, with a class per letter because Seatbelt has no case flag, in three layers where the last match decides: denied at any depth; allowed again inside a dependency or build tree; denied at the workspace's own top folder whatever lies below. Creating or removing `.vscode` is ruled the same way, so it cannot be renamed into place around an `mcp.json`. The layers sit before every other denial, so the allows give back nothing else. Scoped to the workspace, not to caches. The file-tool approval gate keeps asking at any depth, dependency trees included. With 47 names the rules no longer take one pattern a name: every regex is compiled when a command starts, about a quarter of a millisecond each, and one a name took a start from 15 to 40 ms. Names share alternations kept under 900 bytes, because the profile reader refuses a string over 1023 with `Error reading string` (found by trying lengths), which brings a start back to 15 ms with 20 patterns. A parent other tools keep files in, `.config` and `.claude`, is held in place at the workspace's top folder only and never made read-only whole; held at any depth, no command could make a `.config` folder anywhere. The folders loaded as trusted instructions are one list in the crate (`AGENT_INSTRUCTION_PATHS`), which the file tools refuse and the profiles now deny: `.claude/commands/x.md` was refused to `write_file` and writable from `run_command`. Checked with `sandbox-exec`: every new name refused at the top and below it in any case, allowed inside `node_modules`, `.config` not creatable or replaceable at the top and creatable below it, other files in `.claude` and `.config` writable.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Dependency and build trees in the sandbox rule                    | Wire     | Decided in review. `DEPENDENCY_TREE_NAMES` beside the protected list: `node_modules`, `site-packages`, `.venv`, `venv`, `vendor`, `target`, `.pnpm-store`, `.yarn`, `.cargo`, `.gradle`, `Pods`, `bower_components`, and two added on evidence, `.terraform` and `.build`, where Terraform and SwiftPM check out each dependency's whole repository. Published packages ship these file names, so denying them there failed ordinary installs. Sound because a hook manager's files run only from the top of a git repository and a project MCP file loads only at a session's project root, behind consent pinned to its contents.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| A nested `.git` in the sandbox                                    | Keep     | Unchanged by this work: `.git` is denied by path through `git_metadata_paths`, which names the root's `.git` whether it exists or not, the private and shared git directories a linked worktree's `.git` file points to, the `.git` file of each linked worktree checked out below the root, and each submodule's `.git`. It is not denied by name at depth: a rule for any `.git` would stop `git init` and `git clone` below the project, which test suites do. The approval gate still asks for an agent edit of one.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| The submodule map in the sandbox                                  | Wire     | `.gitmodules` at the top of every writable root is denied to sandboxed commands, and bound read-only under bubblewrap when it exists. Submodule `.git` entries were already protected from both `.gitmodules` and the index's gitlinks (`submodule_git_entries`), so removing a line never unprotected a registered submodule. A write denial whose target resolves to a device is limited to the link itself, so a repository that ships `.gitmodules` as a link to `/dev/null` cannot take the null device away from every command.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| A folder that holds a nested checkout                             | Wire     | Only the `.git` entry of a submodule or a linked worktree was denied, so `mv libs/sm aside && mv staged libs/sm` put a folder with a `.git` of the command's own under the name. Each such folder and every folder between it and its root now has creating and removing it denied by literal (`nested_checkout_folders`, `seatbelt_checkout_pins`), after the root pins and before the git denials; its contents stay writable. Checked with `sandbox-exec`: the swap, renaming the parent and removing the folder are refused, and a write inside still works. Under bubblewrap each such folder that exists is bound over itself, writable, ahead of the read-only binds, which makes it a mount point that cannot be renamed or removed; that bind order is reasoned, not run on Linux here. A folder that does not exist yet cannot be bound. A nested checkout whose folder is a link was pinned only where the link leads, so the link itself could be removed and a folder made under its name (run: removed and replaced). It is now pinned under both names.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Name lookups under the sandbox                                    | Wire     | The narrow Seatbelt profile allowed the metadata of the `/var` link only when no developer directory was found. With the network open the resolver reaches its configuration through that link, so `curl https://example.com` failed to resolve while a literal address connected. The rule is now emitted whenever the network is open as well. Checked with `sandbox-exec`: the name resolves, and `/var`, `/private/var/log` and the resolver file stay unreadable. When the Xcode selection link dangles, `host_developer_dir` falls back to the Command Line Tools and names them as `DEVELOPER_DIR`; `git --version`, `python3 -c 1` and `make -v` run under the profile that way. One function, `wrap::seatbelt_environment`, now builds what both profiles and the CLI's shell commands start with: `DEVELOPER_DIR`, and `TMPDIR` as the scratch folder resolves. The shell path set neither, so `git`, `make`, `python3` and `cc` exited with the developer-tools error; and a `TMPDIR` spelled through the `/var` link cannot be reached inside the sandbox, so `xcrun` fell back to `/tmp` and printed a cache error with exit 0.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Post-resolution tests after `resolve_conflict`                    | Wire     | They ran with `tokio::process::Command`, outside the sandbox, under an ordinary command approval that a client may let a page or a phone answer. They now run as `run_command` runs a command: under the sandbox, writing only below the repository, with no network. A session started with `--no-sandbox` runs them directly, as it runs every command.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Protected names in other spellings                                | Wire     | The protected-path gate lower-cased names, which is not what a filesystem that ignores case does. APFS reads `.mcp.json` written with a long s (U+017F), a Kelvin sign for `k` and the `fi` ligature as the same file (checked on an APFS volume), so a not-yet-existing file under such a name was written unasked in a mode that accepts edits. `protected_paths::folded` upper-cases then lower-cases, maps fullwidth forms to ASCII and drops the characters HFS+ ignores. It is exported, and the CLI's instruction-folder check (`path_security::is_agent_instruction_path`) uses it in place of lower-casing, so `commandſ/x.md` under `.claude` is refused like `commands/x.md`. The CLI's other lower-casing of a path segment decides no access: a spelling suggestion, an image extension, a sort key. Not covered: Windows short names, a trailing dot or space, alternate data streams, and compatibility forms other than fullwidth.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Checkpoint patches (`snapshot::apply_patch`)                      | Keep     | Not sent through the patch parser. Its patches are made by host git (`diff-tree -p` between two checkpoint trees, one path at a time) and applied by the same git at the repository's top folder, so no name is read through a prefix and none can leave the work tree. The model and the repository decide the file contents and names in those trees, not the patch's structure, and which sections are reverted is asked for by a client, which the connection rule above limits to the owner.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| What Seatbelt cannot stop                                         | Keep     | A folder prepared where these names are ordinary, in a dependency tree, a cache or the scratch directory, and renamed into the project under an ordinary name carries protected names in below that name. A path rule sees the folder being renamed, not its contents. The workspace's own top-level entries cannot be reached this way. Tracked as `CLI-SANDBOX-PROTECTED-NAME-RENAME-IN-01`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Bubblewrap and protected entries                                  | Keep     | Bubblewrap can only bind an existing path read-only. It binds each top-level entry that exists, `.vscode` whole when it exists, and entries found within three folders and 512 directory reads, not looking into `.git` or a dependency tree. A missing entry is reported once and can be created; case variants and deeper entries are not covered. Every bubblewrap launch, shell commands included, now also hides the credential locations of the user's home that exist at launch (`wrap::bubblewrap_prefix_hiding`: an empty tmpfs over a folder, `/dev/null` over a file); before, only MCP servers had them hidden and a shell command with the network approved could read `~/.ssh`. Not hidden: a credential that is on no list, one created after the command started, and what is in the environment.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| A protected link in a Seatbelt rule                               | Wire     | A protected path or `.git` that is a link is denied as the link and as its target. Naming only the target let a command remove the link and leave its own file there.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
