| Workspace paths holding `"`, `(`, `)` or `\` under Seatbelt       | Wire     | `escape_resolved_seatbelt_path` refused them, so a folder such as `repo-main (1)` could run no sandboxed command. A path is now written as an SBPL string with `\` and `"` escaped; parentheses mean nothing inside a string. A regex is written as an ordinary string, not `#"..."`, which ends at the first quote: `(`, `)` and the other metacharacters each in a class, `\` and `^` escaped, then the same string escaping once. Checked with `sandbox-exec` on a folder named `a (b) "c" \d`: writes inside allowed, beside it denied, `.husky` denied, sibling folders differing in one of the four characters not matched. Control characters, NUL, newlines and U+2028/U+2029 stay refused.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| The policy prompt for a protected file                            | Wire     | When the workspace policy asks and the target is protected, the one prompt is the protected-path prompt: it says why and is rated high risk. It lists every file the call changes, marked `protected:` or `also changes:`, and carries the change itself (the patch as written, a written file's lines, an edit's removed and added lines), since one yes covers the whole call: before, a patch that touched `.mcp.json` and nine other files was approved on the one name, and a hook file without its text. Past 400 lines the prompt says how many are not shown. Bypass has no gate; the mode's hint in the Code view says so.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Credential locations hidden from sandboxed servers                | Wire     | Added `~/.config/gh`, `~/.cargo/credentials(.toml)`, `~/.config/git/credentials`, shell histories, and browser profiles on macOS and Linux (Chrome, Chromium, Edge, Brave, Arc, Firefox, Safari). `~/.claude` and `~/.codex` are named file by file, since both also hold the programs and plugins those tools install: the credential file, settings and configuration, transcripts and prompt history, saved configuration copies, pasted text, attachments and shell snapshots, plus `~/.claude.json`, its copies and Codex's versioned databases found at launch (`credential_copies`). Plans, tasks and caches there stay readable. The list then grew from 49 to 109 with the login files of command-line tools under `~/.local`, `~/.config`, `~/Library` and the home folder: forges, hosting and database platforms, secret managers, package managers' own configuration, infrastructure tools, tunnels, model hubs, database password files and the desktop chat apps' data. Each is the file, or the narrowest folder that holds nothing a program runs from. A package runner that refuses to start when its own configuration file is unreadable would fail under the guarded sandbox, as one that reads `~/.npmrc` already could. Now 111: `~/.zsh_sessions`, where macOS keeps each terminal window's history, and `~/.cursor/mcp.json`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Allowing a request from a query-token connection                  | Cut      | A WebSocket authenticated by `?token=` could allow any pending request, approve a project MCP server, write settings, consent to a skill, enable a plugin, apply a worktree or revert a thread. `developer_sessions::METHOD_EFFECTS` now classifies every protocol method once as read-only, state-changing, trust-granting or a server notification, and such a connection may call a read-only method and nothing else; an unclassified method is refused for it, and a test fails when a method constant has no classification. The five per-method checks are gone. No first-party client connects that way: the Electron shell and the VS Code extension each spawn `agi app-server` on stdio, a paired phone talks to the shell, and the terminal UI runs in process. `--allow-query-token` is off by default and now gives a read-only connection. `mcp/approval/read` is classified apart (`ReadsForOwner`) and refused to it: it returns the arguments and URLs of a launch waiting for approval. What a read-only connection still sees, and the token is in a URL that gets logged: every notification, approval text included, every thread of the workspace, checkpoint diffs, the hooks, and the account's email and credits. `run_ws`, a second WebSocket entry point nothing called and which gave every connection the owner's trust, is cut with its transport variant. `account/status` and `model/list` from such a connection have `refresh` set to false before they reach the host, which cannot see who asked: a refresh drops the cached account and calls the hosted API with the owner's credential, or probes every local runtime and looks up a credential per route. No other read-only method takes a flag; `thread/list` and `context/instructions` take a folder to read from, which the host holds to its own workspace for every connection. Stripping `refresh` was not enough for `model/list`, whose host probes on its first call whatever the flag says, and `mcp/list` read the keychain for each server, so the host methods behind both now take the calling connection's trust (`list_local_models(params, trust)`, `list_mcp_servers(trust)`) and answer one that proved nothing from what is already resolved. Detached requests, which run host git over a checkout, are bounded: 8 a connection and 16 in the process, of which a read-only connection gets 2 and all read-only connections 4; one that finds no slot is refused at once and nothing queues.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `agiworkforce-protocol` `PROTECTED_METADATA_PATH_NAMES`           | Keep     | Not a copy of the sandbox crate's list. It belongs to the permission-profile wire model in `agiworkforce-protocol`: `FileSystemSandboxPolicy::workspace_write`, `default_read_only_subpaths_for_writable_root` and `protected_metadata_names_for_writable_root` read it to fill the read-only subpaths and metadata names of a `WritableRoot` in the types exported to TypeScript. Its set is that model's own (`.git`, `.agents`, `.agiworkforce`, `.codex`), and nothing outside the crate enforces from it. The TypeScript list in `packages/contracts/local-runtime` is the mirror, held to the Rust one by a parity test.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Endpoint, socket and key-log names in a launch consent            | Wire     | A name is a credential's only when its last word says so, words split at `_` and a trailing number not counted (`DB_PASS`, `MYSQL_PWD`, `API_KEY_2`), the long words also at the end of that word (`PGPASSWORD`), or when it is one of two listed names (`DOCKER_AUTH_CONFIG`, `GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS_JSON`) or starts a listed family (`TF_TOKEN_`, `POETRY_PYPI_TOKEN_`, `NuGetPackageSourceCredentials_`). A value that may be a setting or may carry a credential, with nothing in the name to tell, is hidden and pinned by a digest: `GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_<n>` and `GIT_CONFIG_PARAMETERS` (`GIT_CONFIG_COUNT` and `GIT_CONFIG_KEY_<n>` stay shown and pinned, so all three of the family re-ask), any name ending `_HEADER` or `_HEADERS` and the bare `HEADER` that `--header` names, and Bundler's per-host names (`BUNDLE_…__…`). Before, a `GIT_CONFIG_VALUE_0` holding an `Authorization` line was shown whole. `PWD` and `OLDPWD` are the working directory, and `DANGEROUSLY_OMIT_AUTH`, `NPM_CONFIG_ALWAYS_AUTH` and `SECURE_COOKIE` are switches: all five are listed and shown and pinned whatever they hold. `MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS` and `MAX_KEYS` are limits, so a plural with a count word beside it only might be a credential. And under any other name a switch value (`true`, `false`, `0`, `1`, `yes`, `no`, `on`, `off` or nothing) is shown and pinned, so `SKIP_AUTH=true` cannot be flipped unasked. The npm, pip, Cargo and Docker families were checked name by name in a test: their tokens and passwords are neither shown nor hashed, and their config files, certificate files, wrappers and switches are pinned. A secret word earlier in a name leaves the value hidden and pinned by a digest (`AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `SESSION_ID`, `TOKEN_TTL`): before, any such word left it unpinned, so `ANSIBLE_HOST_KEY_CHECKING`, `OIDC_TOKEN_ENDPOINT` and `AUTH_HOST` could change unasked. A substring is no longer enough to leave a value unpinned: `NODE_TLS_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED` held `AUTH` and could be switched unasked. `BEHAVIOUR_NAMES` lists switches and commands that are always shown and pinned (`PATH`, `NODE_OPTIONS`, `NODE_TLS_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED`, `PYTHONSTARTUP`, `LD_PRELOAD`, `DYLD_INSERT_LIBRARIES`, `GOAUTH`, `GIT_SSH_COMMAND`, `GIT_ASKPASS`, `SSH_ASKPASS`, `SSH_ASKPASS_REQUIRE`, `SUDO_ASKPASS`, `BORG_PASSCOMMAND`, `SESSION_MANAGER`). Endings that are shown and pinned whole, whatever words come before: `_SOCK`, `_DIR`, `_DIRECTORY`, the trust files `_CAFILE`, `_CAINFO`, `_CAPATH`, `_BUNDLE`, `_CERT`, `_CERTS`, and the switches `_CHECKING`, `_VERIFY`, `_VERIFICATION`, `_INSECURE`, `_UNAUTHORIZED`; by name, `SSLKEYLOGFILE`, `XAUTHORITY`, `PGPASSFILE` and `GIT_SSL_KEY`. `_ENDPOINT`, `_HOST`, `_SERVER`, `_ADDR`, `_ADDRESS`, `_PROXY`, `_REGISTRY` and `_INDEX` are endpoints, shown as where they point, since a proxy or a registry address often carries a password. |
| What a consent records for an endpoint                            | Wire     | `launch_env` decides two things apart: `shown_value` is what the approval question displays and `pinned_value` is what a change re-asks for. An endpoint by name is shown as scheme, host, port and path, with `(credentials hidden)` when userinfo, a query or a fragment was dropped. It is pinned as that plus a digest of the query and fragment with the value of each credential parameter blanked: `postgres://db/main?host=other&sslmode=disable` showed and pinned like `postgres://db/main`, so a host or a TLS option could be moved into the query unasked. A rotated `password=` or `token=` still does not ask again; a parameter whose name is not a credential's does, a signature included. The user name before the host is not pinned. What cannot be shown is still pinned, by a SHA-256 the caller computes: an endpoint that does not parse, such as two index URLs in one value, and a name that holds a secret word only inside a longer one. A credential by name is neither shown nor pinned, so rotating a token does not ask again and no digest of a short password is stored; a credential inside a digest-pinned value does ask again when it rotates. A token carried in the path of an endpoint is still shown.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| A launch file that cannot be pinned                               | Wire     | `launch_files::pinned_launch_file_digests` read each file whole after an `is_file` check, so a FIFO or a very large file left by a sandboxed command could hang the host or exhaust its memory, and a file it could not read dropped out of the pins unnoticed. Each file is now opened without blocking and without following a link (`wrap::open_regular`, shared with `read_regular_file`) and read to `PINNED_FILE_SIZE_LIMIT` (64 MiB; the largest single-file entries found installed were 14 and 9 MiB) for at most `PINNED_FILE_COUNT_LIMIT` (64) files. One that cannot be pinned stays in the map as `UNPINNABLE_PREFIX` (`unpinnable:`) and the reason; an ordinary file's digest is unchanged. In the CLI such a launch hashes with a value that is new at every start, so an approval recorded for it never matches again, and the question and the launch's file list name the file and the reason. The dead fingerprint is still written to the consent file; no separate in-memory store was added.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| A launch through a shell or a wrapper                             | Wire     | `sh -c "node x.js"`, `npx tsx x.ts` and `env A=1 node x.js` pinned no file and were not marked, so an edit to that code never asked again. `launch_files` now looks through what stands in front: `env` with assignments, a shell's `-c`, `cmd /c`, PowerShell's `-Command`, and the package runners (`npx`, `pnpx`, `bunx`, `uvx`, `npm exec`, `pnpm dlx` and `exec`, `yarn dlx` and `exec`, `bun x`), four deep, and reads what they start as a launch of its own. A command a shell is given is read only when it is plain words and its first word is a path or a tool the file knows; `cd`, `.`, `eval` and `trap` are the shell's own and run code no command line shows. Anything else is marked as a file that could not be pinned is, under the part that makes it so (`sh -c`, `node -e`, `python3 -c`, `deno eval`, `powershell -EncodedCommand`, `env -S`, `npx -c`), and asks at every start in both hosts. A node package runner also lists `node_modules/.bin/<program>`, which it runs in place of the fetched package when the project has one. Not covered: a program name that differs from its package's, a workspace root's `node_modules` above the launch folder, `uv run python x.py`, and `sh -c` around a program the file does not know, which asks every time.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| Credential files read through a link                              | Wire     | `read_file` checked the name it was given and `read_many_files` checked nothing, so a link named `notes.txt` to `.env` was read. `path_security::reads_a_credential_file` judges the name and where it resolves, each as written and folded, and `read_file`, `read_many_files` and the content search use it. `mentions.rs` and `repo/index_policy.rs` still judge the name alone and belong to other lanes.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Instruction folders and `resolve_conflict`                        | Wire     | `merge_conflicts::validate_conflict_path` skipped the instruction-folder denylist the file tools apply; both now call `path_security::instruction_folder_refusal`. The read of a git configuration file for its excludes is bounded by the crate's reader.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| The profile a command runs under                                  | Wire     | `run_command` ran under the profile that also confines reads, so nothing installed in the home folder could start: `~/.cargo/bin/rustfmt --version` failed with `Operation not permitted`, and so would any rustup, nvm or pyenv tool. It now runs under the full-read profile with the credential locations denied (`full_read::command_seatbelt_profile`), as MCP servers do, and with the manager's own write policy, so a read-only session still writes only its scratch folder. Run: rustfmt and cargo start, the 111 credential locations that exist are unreadable, every protected write is refused. Bubblewrap already read everywhere with the same locations masked.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| Listening under an internet-only sandbox                          | Wire     | `(allow network-inbound)` let a command held to the internet open a TCP port on `0.0.0.0`. It is narrowed to `(allow network-inbound (local udp "*:*"))`: a program that asks a name server directly has to bind a UDP socket before it can send. Run: a TCP listener on `0.0.0.0` and `127.0.0.1` is refused; a name lookup, a UDP query to `1.1.1.1`, `curl` and `git ls-remote` over HTTPS work. A full-network sandbox still listens. Left: a UDP socket can receive from anyone.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| What a sandboxed command inherits                                 | Keep     | Decided by the lead after the source was read: Codex CLI's `shell_environment_policy` inherits the whole environment and keeps names holding `KEY`, `SECRET` and `TOKEN` unless `ignore_default_excludes` is set to false (its configuration reference and `config_types.rs`, read 2026-10-11), and a trusted workspace here does the same, so `env` in a sandboxed command shows the tokens the session was started with. An untrusted workspace, a managed policy and `AGIWORKFORCE_SANDBOX_SCRUB_ENV` still give only the short allowlist. New and off by default: `AGIWORKFORCE_SANDBOX_FILTER_ENV` leaves out what the launch classifier calls a credential (`holds_a_credential`: the name says so and the value is not a switch), and `AGIWORKFORCE_SANDBOX_PASS_ENV=NAME,NAME` passes named ones through it. `sandbox::apply_sandboxed_environment` is the one function that applies the resolved policy to a child; the executors call it, so a plugin hook gets the rule `run_command` gets. Tracked as `CLI-SANDBOX-ENVIRONMENT-INHERITED-01`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Host programs the tools run by name                               | Cut      | `grep_files` and `search_files` started `rg` and `grep` by bare name, and the session context ran `--version` on whatever `PATH` held, on the host and without asking; a project's `.venv/bin` or `node_modules/.bin` is often first on `PATH` and writable from the sandbox. The search programs are found through `path_security::host_program`, which looks only in folders no sandbox writes, and a program inside the project, a workspace root or a temporary folder is listed without being run for its version. A session started in the home folder keeps the toolchains under home trusted, since the sandbox writes a private workspace there.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| What an edit prompt shows                                         | Wire     | A patch prompt carried its first 40 lines with no list of files and no sign of more, a write or multi-edit 40 lines of diff, an edit three lines of each side, and a new file only its length. Every prompt now lists each file the answer covers first and then the whole change to the 400 lines the protected-path prompt already used (`common::change_detail`), with a line that says how many more there are; a new file is shown as its lines. Reading a long one needs the scrolling overlay, which is the prompts lane's.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| What a launch pins beyond the file it names                       | Wire     | Unpinned and unmarked before: the settings a package runner reads first, `node --env-file`, `tsx watch`, recipe runners, containers, and a variable set on the command line. A node package runner and bun now pin the launch folder's `.npmrc`, `.yarnrc.yml` and `bunfig.toml`; `--env-file` and `--env-file-if-exists` are code options; `tsx watch <entry>` is read like `bun run <entry>`. `make`, `just` and `deno task` pin the file their recipes are read from (the makefile names, `justfile`, `deno.json`, or the one `-f` names), as `npm run` pins `package.json`, which was chosen over marking them unchecked. A container is marked unchecked only when it runs what this machine holds: `docker` or `podman` with `compose`, or `run` with `-v`, `--volume`, `--mount` or `--volumes-from`; an image alone runs what the image holds, as a fetched package does, and marking every `docker run` would ask at every start of the most common kind of server. A variable set through `env` or a shell prefix (`env NODE_OPTIONS=... node x.js`) cannot be withheld as an entry's variable is, so `pinned_launch_file_digests_holding` and `unchecked_launch_holding` take the host's own test of which names load code and mark such a launch; the plain functions hold nothing, so a host has to switch to them.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| A patch that moves a submodule                                    | Cut      | `git apply` printed a submodule pointer as changed, exited 0 and changed nothing, and the tool reported it applied; a new gitlink made an empty folder. A patch whose header gives a file mode 160000, or whose target is a folder, is refused before git is asked to apply anything.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| One reader for a file the project controls                        | Wire     | `agiworkforce_sandbox_policy::read_file_within(root, relative, limit, holds_credentials)` reads a file whoever writes the folder chooses, such as instructions, a rule or a skill: only when the place it really is, every link followed, is a regular file inside the root, and only when it holds no more than the limit. It opens where the path resolved without following a link and without blocking, on the crate's `open_regular`, which stays private. It says why it read nothing (`NotRead`: missing, outside, not a regular file, too large, unreadable), so a caller can still log a link that leads out. Staying inside the root was the only test, so `.agiworkforce/rules/a.md` linked to the project's own `.env` loaded as a rule and reached a read-only connection through `settings/read`. The reader now asks the caller's credential-name test about the name it was given and the name it lands on, each as a path below the root, and answers `NotRead::Credential`; the CLI passes `path_security::names_a_credential_file`, the policy `read_file` applies, since the crate has no pattern engine to hold that list itself. Not caught: a folder above the file swapped for a link between the check and the open. `apps/cli/src/project_text.rs` is to switch to it and drop its own open.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |

## 2026-10-11: Tauri command and event shapes

Retained Tauri code and its renderer. A comparison of what each `invoke` sends and reads with what each Rust command takes and returns found pairs that disagreed on field names and failed without an error. Every Wire and Cut row has a test beside the code that changed.

| Capability                                   | Decision | Evidence and retained path                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| -------------------------------------------- | -------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Cloud storage command arguments              | Wire     | `src/stores/cloudStore.ts` sends `config` and `request` with camelCase keys. `CloudOAuthConfig` (`integrations/cloud/mod.rs`) and the six request structs in `sys/commands/cloud.rs` read snake_case, so every cloud command that takes a struct failed to deserialize. They now carry `rename_all = "camelCase"`. None of them is read from stored data, so there is no alias. Replies stay snake_case, which is what the store reads.                                                                                                                                                  |
| MCP sign-in replies                          | Wire     | `src/api/mcp.ts` read `auth_url`, `user_info`, `avatar_url` and `expires_at` from replies `sys/commands/mcp_oauth.rs` sends in camelCase: the sign-in page opened `undefined` and a connected account had no name. The six readers and the four fixtures in `src/lib/tauri-mock.ts` use the sent names.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Star on a local project                      | Wire     | `project_update` dropped `isStarred`, so a star was gone after a reload. It is kept in the existing `projects.metadata` JSON column (schema v69) under `starred`, the key the managed-cloud project record uses, and `project_list` returns `isStarred`. No migration.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Single error report from the renderer        | Wire     | `src/stores/ui.ts` sent `errorType` and `stackTrace` to `error_report`, which reads `error_type` and `stack_trace`. It now sends the names the batch path in `src/services/errorReporting.ts` already used.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Local analytics event                        | Wire     | `src/services/analytics.ts` sent `sessionId` and `userId`; `TelemetryEvent` in `sys/telemetry/collector.rs` reads `session_id` and `user_id` and is also the shape of `analytics_events.json`, so the renderer changed, not the struct.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Whisper model list                           | Wire     | `voice_list_whisper_models` sends `approximate_size_mb`, `is_downloaded` and `path`. Voice settings read `sizeBytes` and `downloaded`, showed "(NaN MB)" and never showed a model as downloaded. The renderer type in `src/api/voice.ts` now matches the reply.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| MCP server tool count                        | Wire     | `mcp_list_servers` sends `toolCount`; `src/types/mcp.ts` named it `tool_count`, so the connector health list showed "undefined tools".                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `shortcut_registered` with an action name    | Cut      | The system-level registration in `sys/commands/shortcuts.rs` emitted the event with the bare action string, and the store added that string to its shortcut list. Removed; `shortcuts_register` emits the shortcut record, the only payload the store handles.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Approval kind on `agent:permission_required` | Wire     | The handler in `src/stores/chat/agentWorkflowEvents.ts` read a `type` the envelope from `core/agent/approval.rs` never carried, so every approval was labelled a terminal command. The kind now comes from `scope.type`: terminal, filesystem (data modification), anything else (tool execution). An MCP-scoped request is not `mcp_tool`, because that kind is answered through the tool-confirmation command. The deadline fields stay unread: the renderer times an approval from settings.                                                                                          |
| Extension status sent to the renderer        | Wire     | `ExtensionStatus` serialised as `lowercase` (`pendingremoval`) while the database and the settings page use `pending_removal`. It serialises as snake_case, matching its `Display`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| Event type in `get_audit_events`             | Wire     | Every row was read back as `AuditEventType::Other(stored)`, which serialised as an object; the audit list in Settings, Privacy renders and searches it as text. The type serialises as its plain string and a stored string maps back to its variant. Signing and storage already used `as_str()`, so no signature changes.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `userId` on `chat_set_monthly_budget`        | Cut      | The command takes `amount` only; the budget is one setting for the local profile. `src/stores/billing/costSlice.ts` no longer sends the extra argument.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Shortcut `is_global` in the renderer type    | Wire     | `src/stores/shortcutStore.ts` named it `isGlobal`; the records come from Rust as `is_global`. Type, selector and fixture renamed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Shape mismatches with no reader              | Keep     | Left as they are because no mounted component reads the wrong-cased field or makes the call: ROI, process, user, tool, system and app metric types (`src/api/analytics.ts`), `src/api/productivity.ts`, `src/api/database.ts`, provider status and usage types (`src/stores/modelStore.ts`), the cost types on `chatStore`, the goal list type, the dotfiles client type, `DockPosition` `top` and `bottom`, governance `timedout` (only pending requests are listed), the renderer-only fields in `settings_save`, and `conversationCount` and `defaultPrivacyMode` on a local project. |

## 2026-10-11: Tauri trigger engine (cron, webhook, file watcher)

Retained Tauri code only. Applies to `core/agent/triggers.rs` and the builder in `src/features/workflows/AutomationBuilder.tsx`. The limits below are named constants in `triggers.rs`; the builder reads the ones it states from `trigger_engine_info`.

| Capability                               | Decision | Evidence and retained path                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| ---------------------------------------- | -------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| One live run per trigger                 | Wire     | Every firing goes through `TriggerEngine::fire`. A firing that arrives while the trigger's own background agent is queued, running or parked for approval is counted on the trigger (`coalescedCount`) and not started, and no run is queued for it afterwards. The next run that starts is told how many firings were merged.                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Minimum interval between starts          | Wire     | `DEFAULT_MIN_START_INTERVAL_SECS`, 60 seconds, for webhook and file-watcher triggers; a firing inside it is counted the same way. Cron is exempt because its own five-minute minimum already spaces its slots, and it is still held to one live run. The builder states the rule and the number.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Files the app writes itself              | Cut      | A watcher drops every path under the app's data, config, cache and log folders and `~/.agiworkforce`, every credential path the shared denylist names, and any change reported within five seconds of the trigger's own run ending. A folder that is one of those, or broad enough to contain one (the home folder), is refused as a watch root.                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Webhook request read                     | Wire     | The listener read one packet into 64 KB, so a request split across packets lost its body. It now reads headers to the blank line (16 KiB at most, else 431), checks the method, origin, path and token, and only then reads exactly `Content-Length` bytes (256 KiB at most, else 413). A chunked body is refused with 411, conflicting lengths or a body cut short with 400, and the whole read has ten seconds (408). At most 32 connections are served at once.                                                                                 |
| Webhook paths                            | Wire     | Every new webhook shared the default `/webhook` and only the first was reachable. A webhook created without a path gets `/hooks/<random>`; a path another trigger uses is refused at create, at edit and when turning a trigger on, naming the owner. Stored rows that share a path keep one owner and the others are turned off with the reason; a request to a still-shared path goes to the trigger whose token it carries.                                                                                                                     |
| Webhook address and token in the builder | Wire     | The card shows the full address and the token dialog shows the address, the `Authorization: Bearer` header and a `curl` example. The listener binds 127.0.0.1 only and the token is compared as SHA-256 digests in constant time; both were already so and are unchanged.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Webhook listener that cannot bind        | Wire     | A failed bind was logged once and the listener never started. It now retries every 30 seconds, every webhook trigger shows the reason until it is up, and one notification is sent when a webhook that is turned on is waiting.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Webhook replay                           | Wire     | Chosen: an optional `Idempotency-Key` header. A repeat of the same key to the same trigger within ten minutes is answered 200 with `duplicate` and starts nothing. A request without the header is not deduplicated by its body, because two identical deliveries can be two real events; the minimum interval still merges a burst.                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| File watcher on toggle                   | Wire     | Turning a watcher on started nothing until the next restart, and turning it off left it watching. `toggle_trigger` now starts and stops it. A watcher that cannot start is left off and the switch says why.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| File pattern                             | Wire     | An invalid pattern matched every file and a valid one was matched against the file name only. An invalid pattern is refused at create and edit, and the pattern is matched against the path below the watched folder, so `*.md` is the folder itself and `**/*.md` is every level. A stored `*.md` that used to match at every level now matches the top folder only.                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Watched folder                           | Wire     | The renderer could name any folder outside a short denylist. A folder is accepted only when it came from `pick_trigger_watch_folder` (the native dialog, opened in Rust) this session, is inside a folder already granted to the agent, or is the folder the trigger was already approved for. A stored watcher from an older build whose folder is not granted is turned off until its folder is chosen again. Links are not followed out of the folder: each reported path is resolved and dropped unless it is really inside.                   |
| Cron catch-up                            | Wire     | One policy, stated in the builder: each trigger stores the instant its schedule is settled to. On start or wake, the slots that passed since then make one run, and a notification says how many were missed. Creating, turning on or rescheduling a trigger settles it at that moment, so no slot in the past ever runs.                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Run count and last run                   | Wire     | Both were memory only and read zero after a restart. They are stored with the trigger row after each firing, in the existing `payload` JSON; no migration.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| Weekday ranges                           | Wire     | `6-7` and `1-7` were refused because the cron library numbers Sunday 1 and rejects a range ending on Sunday. A five-field weekday field is now expanded into the library's own day list: lists, ranges, steps and names, with 0 and 7 both Sunday. The translation is the shared `cron_schedule` in `core/scheduler/schedule.rs`, which scheduled tasks use too; the trigger engine's own copy is deleted.                                                                                                                                         |
| Trigger time zone                        | Wire     | `timezone` was stored and never read; every schedule ran in UTC while the form implied otherwise. Triggers now follow the same rule as scheduled tasks, through `schedule_zone` and `next_run` in `core/scheduler/schedule.rs`: a named IANA zone on its wall clock, an unknown name refused, and no name meaning this computer's time zone. A schedule stored before zones were read is given UTC by name when it is loaded, so it keeps running when it did. New triggers default to the computer's zone and the builder states the zone in use. |
| Webhook delivery that will not run       | Wire     | A delivery held back by the minimum interval or a live run was answered 202 and kept its `Idempotency-Key`, so the sender's retry was answered as a duplicate and the payload never ran. It is now answered 429 with `Retry-After` (the rest of the interval, or 30 seconds for a live run) and its key is forgotten, so the retry is judged fresh. It is still counted on the trigger.                                                                                                                                                            |
| Trigger model field                      | Cut      | `TriggerAction.model` was saved and never read once the picker was cut. The field is removed from the struct, the renderer type and what an edit sends. A stored row that still names a model loads and drops it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Trigger save failures                    | Wire     | A failed write was only logged, so create, edit, toggle, token regeneration and delete reported success and reverted at relaunch. Each now writes first and changes memory after, and returns the error to the builder. The engine's own bookkeeping (counters, the settled instant, a trigger turned off because it cannot work) logs instead, since it has no caller to refuse.                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Firing log in the builder                | Wire     | Every row showed a duration of NaN, called a started run a success, and never reloaded once it had a row. Rows now read Started, Notified or Could not start, with what has become of the run read from the background agents when the log is opened, and the log reloads each time it is opened.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Edit form reset                          | Wire     | The edit form was rebuilt from the stored trigger on every re-render of the page, discarding what was typed. It now resets only when the dialog opens or the edited trigger changes.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| Approval and live-run wording            | Wire     | One rule, stated where it applies: a trigger has one run at a time, a run parked for approval counts, and later firings are counted, not started. The card says so while a run is going or waiting.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
