## 2026-10-11: Cloud refresh token stays in the host

Retained Tauri code and the Electron shell. Applies to `sys/account/mod.rs`, `electron/accountBridge.ts` and the shared `src/services/cloudAccountAuth.ts`.

| Capability                                    | Decision | Evidence and retained path                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| --------------------------------------------- | -------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Refresh token in the page                     | Cut      | Both hosts returned the raw device-token reply to the page at sign-in and returned the stored refresh token again at every launch (`account_restore_refresh_token`), and the page kept it in its session and did the refresh itself. The host now takes the refresh token out of the sign-in reply and keeps it; `account_restore_refresh_token` and `account_store_refresh_token` are deleted on both hosts. The page holds the access token only.                                                    |
| Session renewal                               | Wire     | `account_refresh_session` does the exchange in the host against the registered Cloud origin: sent once, no redirect followed, one exchange at a time, the rotated token saved before the access token is returned. Tauri uses a new `ApiClient::single_attempt_without_redirects()`, because the shared single-attempt client follows redirects and a followed 307 or 308 would replay the token.                                                                                                      |
| Renewal outcomes                              | Wire     | The host answers renewed, rejected, unreachable or signed out. Rejected is 400, 401 or 403, or a renewal that came back for another account: the saved session is cleared and the page signs out. Unreachable is a network failure, a timeout, a redirect, 429, 5xx or a reply that cannot be read: nothing is cleared, the page keeps the sign-in with a notice, and the next need tries again, at most once in 30 seconds. A network error used to sign the person out and delete the saved session. |
| Sign-in that is abandoned, and account switch | Wire     | The refresh token from a sign-in is held until the page adopts the access token it came with, so an abandoned sign-in cannot replace the saved account. An access token for another account or sign-in that brought no refresh token makes the host forget the saved one. A renewal that finishes after a sign-out or a switch is discarded.                                                                                                                                                           |

## 2026-10-11: what the Tauri window can make the host do

Retained Tauri code and its renderer. The window renders model output, so a command it can call is a command a script in it can call. These are the computer-use, browser, capture and recording commands that acted with no check the window could not answer for itself.

| Capability                                                 | Decision | Evidence and retained path                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| ---------------------------------------------------------- | -------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Allowing an app for computer use from the window           | Wire     | `app_permissions_set` and `app_permissions_remove` (`sys/commands/computer_use.rs`) stored whatever the window sent, so a script in it could allow any app. A change that lets an app be used more than its stored state (to allowed, or off a block) now takes a native yes that names the app; a change that restricts it is stored as sent. On macOS the app has to be running and the name and bundle id shown and stored are the ones this process reads for it, so a request cannot pair one app's name with another's identifier. `AppPermissionManager::loosens` compares the request with what both the name and the bundle id have stored.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| Handing screen control back                                | Wire     | `computer_use_hand_back` resumed control for any caller. A click on Hand back reaches the command exactly as a script would and Rust cannot tell them apart, so resuming after a takeover takes a native yes. Nothing is asked when the screen was not taken over.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| 42 browser commands with no caller                         | Cut      | No mounted component, command-client wrapper or Rust code called them, and each drove or read the controlled browser for whatever ran in the window; the three script commands asked through a confirmation the window itself could answer. Removed with their helpers from `sys/commands/browser.rs`, `src/api/browser.ts`, `src/stores/browserStore.ts`, `src/lib/browserAutomation.ts` and both command lists: `browser_close_tab`, `browser_switch_tab`, `browser_list_tabs`, `browser_wait_for_navigation`, `browser_click`, `browser_type`, `browser_get_text`, `browser_get_attribute`, `browser_wait_for_selector`, `browser_select_option`, `browser_check`, `browser_uncheck`, `browser_hover`, `browser_focus`, `browser_query_all`, `browser_scroll_into_view`, `browser_get_element_state`, `browser_wait_for_interactive`, `browser_fill_form`, `browser_drag_and_drop`, `browser_upload_file`, `browser_screenshot`, `browser_get_content`, `browser_get_dom_snapshot`, `browser_highlight_element`, `browser_evaluate`, `browser_execute_async_js`, `browser_call_function`, `browser_execute_in_frame`, `browser_get_cookies`, `browser_set_cookie`, `browser_clear_cookies`, `browser_get_performance_metrics`, `browser_get_frames`, `find_element_semantic`, `find_all_elements_semantic`, `click_semantic`, `type_semantic`, `get_accessibility_tree`, `get_dom_semantic_graph`, `get_interactive_elements`, `find_by_role`. The model's browser tools never used these wrappers; they call `BrowserStateWrapper` and `DomOperations` directly, and both are untouched. |
| Launch flags, profile folder and proxy on `browser_launch` | Cut      | The command took `args`, `userDataDir`, `profileName` and `proxy` from the window, enough to start a browser on the user's own profile or with a flag that runs a program. The viewer never sent any of them. It takes the browser type and headless only.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Browser viewer commands                                    | Wire     | The nine that start, steer or look at the controlled browser (`browser_launch`, `browser_open_tab`, `browser_navigate`, `browser_go_back`, `browser_go_forward`, `browser_reload`, `browser_get_url`, `browser_get_title`, `browser_get_screenshot_stream`) refuse while the Browser switch in Settings, Capabilities is off, which the window cannot turn on by itself. There is no browser consent state in this app; the model's browser tools answer to the same switch. `browser_init`, `browser_check_status` and `browser_close` report or close and ask nothing.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Screen capture from the window                             | Wire     | `capture_screen_full`, `capture_screen_region`, `capture_screen_window` and `capture_get_windows` (`sys/commands/capture.rs`) checked nothing. Each refuses while the Screenshots switch is off, and the first one of an app run takes a native yes of its own, kept until the app quits. That yes is not computer-use consent and grants nothing else; a capture taken for a model still requires live computer-use consent in `automation/computer_use/session.rs`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Recording clicks and keystrokes                            | Wire     | `automation_record_start` started a recorder of every click and key in every app and handed the result to the window, with no check. Starting one takes a native yes that says typed text is recorded, passwords included. `automation_record_stop` and `automation_record_get_last` hand back only the session and the recording that yes produced; a running session it did not start is discarded.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `src/lib/browserAutomation.ts`                             | Keep     | Unreachable from the app, and named by `scripts/check-module-reachability.mjs`, `scripts/config/surface-reachability-allowlist.json` and one cell of `docs/product/surface-feature-matrix.json`. Trimmed to the twelve commands that remain so it calls nothing that is gone; deleting it means changing those three with it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |

## 2026-10-11: CLI hooks, plugin pins and approval prompts

| Capability                               | Decision | Evidence and retained path                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| ---------------------------------------- | -------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Hook `args`                              | Wire     | `Hook.args` was parsed and never read, so `{"command":"sh","args":["guard.sh"]}` ran a bare `sh` that took the hook's JSON on stdin as its script, and `$(...)` in a tool argument ran. A hook with `args` is a program launch (`HookLaunch::Program` in `features/hooks/hooks.rs`): on the host and in the user-hook sandbox the program is started with exactly those arguments; a plugin hook reaches its sandbox through the existing shell entry with each argument quoted by `sandbox::shell_join`, the same quoting that entry already applies to its own cache wrapper. A `command` with no `args` stays a shell command line. `Hook::display_command` is the one way a hook is named, arguments included, in `hooks list`, failure messages, the rewrite audit log and the managed `allowedCommands` match. |
| Hook event names in a hooks file         | Wire     | The keys of `hooks` were kept as written while `run_hooks` looked up `HookEvent`'s `Display`, so a hook under `BeforeToolUse`, `AfterToolUse` or any other older name never ran, and the empty-list help printed `AfterToolUse` as its example. The canonical spelling of an event is its variant name. `deserialize_hooks_by_event` maps the ten older names in `LEGACY_EVENT_ALIASES` to it, keeping the order the file gives, and refuses any other key with an error that names it and lists both sets. Plugin hooks take the same mapping in `append_plugin_hooks`, which reports a bad entry and leaves it out instead of failing the user's own hooks.                                                                                                                                                        |
| A hook that could never fire             | Wire     | A `matcher` that is not a regular expression, an `if` that is not `Tool(pattern)` and an empty `command` were skipped at run time without a word. `validate_hook` refuses each when the hooks file, a plugin or the managed policy loads, naming the hook.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `[async]` in the hooks list              | Cut      | No hook runs asynchronously: every hook is awaited, and `blocking: false` only means that its failure does not stop the hooks after it and that the action is not blocked when it cannot start. The list says `[non-blocking]` and the field says what it does.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `--trust-plugin`                         | Cut      | Named in the message for a blocked project-plugin hook and defined nowhere. The message names the route that exists, `agi plugin install <path>`. Trusting a folder a sandboxed command can write needs a content pin and its own decision.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `--integrity sha256:` for a git source   | Wire     | `hash_dir_tree_sha256` hashed `.git`, which differs between two clones of one commit, so no pin could match. `plugins::plugin_content_sha256` hashes what git tracks: every file and link below the plugin folder except `.git`, in path order, each as its git mode (100644, 100755 or 120000), its path, its length and its bytes, a link's bytes being its target. A mismatch says what the digest covers, the `--integrity` help says it too, and `agi plugin digest <path>` prints the digest. A local install copies a link as a link, so the installed folder hashes as its source does.                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| Keeping a marketplace update             | Wire     | `marketplace update` pulled, checked the signature and kept the result, so new hooks ran unasked and nothing compared the plugin with what had been agreed to. `Marketplace::update_all_with` shows a `PluginUpdateReview` for each pull that changed anything: the files changed, every hook, MCP server, skill, command and agent added or changed (`plugins::unapproved_capabilities`, fingerprinted with their arguments and file bytes), and the new content digest beside the one on record. The update stays only on a yes at the terminal; a run that cannot ask keeps nothing. Otherwise the plugin is reset to the commit agreed to. `InstalledPluginEntry.content_sha256` records the digest at install and at each kept update.                                                                          |
| A run that ends at the question          | Wire     | `plugins::pending_update_marker`, a file beside the plugin folder holding the approved commit, is written before the pull and removed when the update is kept or put back. `PluginsManager::load_from_dir` loads a plugin with that file disabled, so an interrupted update contributes no hook, server or skill, and the next `marketplace update` asks about it even when there is nothing new to pull.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Approval prompt taller than the terminal | Wire     | `ApprovalOverlayState::render_into` gave the box a fixed height and no key scrolled it, so a long change lost its tail and its buttons while `y` still approved. The question, the targets (`targets_for`, from the typed request) and the buttons are always drawn, buttons wrapping in a narrow window; the detail scrolls on the arrows, `j`/`k`, PageUp, PageDown, Home and End with a `lines 1-15 of 212` row; and `tui/widgets/scroll_gate.rs` holds every allowing answer until a draw has painted the last row. A prompt that has not been drawn, or a window too small for the buttons, takes no allowing answer. A refusing key always answers.                                                                                                                                                            |
| MCP elicitation and diff review          | Wire     | The elicitation modal was drawn through `render_overlay(.., 0)`, cut at the bottom with Accept reachable and a long URL ellipsised; `/diff-review` approved a file on the first three lines of its diff and its base name. Both use the same gate: `ElicitationOverlayState::render_into` scrolls the message, the whole URL and the fields above the buttons and follows the focused field; `DiffReviewView` shows the full path and the whole diff of the file under the cursor and fits itself to the rows `InteractiveView::fit_rows` reports.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
